Allen Visual Behavior Ophys data to standardized pickle format.\n\nUsage:\n    python -u convert_data.py <output_path.pkl>\n    python -u convert_data.py <output_path.pkl> --sample\n\nData: All mice from the VisualBehavior project (single-plane ophys).\nEach session has one imaging plane. Trials are segmented using the\nbuilt-in trials table (start_time to stop_time, variable length).\n\nOutput variables (decoded from neural activity):\n  - running_speed: percentile-binned across all sessions (time-varying)\n  - pupil_diameter: percentile-binned across all sessions (time-varying)\n  - image_name: categorical image on screen (time-varying, changes at change_time)\n  - image_change: binary, 0 before change_time, 1 at/after (time-varying)\n  - trial_outcome: categorical hit/miss/false_alarm/correct_reject (per-trial)\n\"\"\"\n\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport argparse\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport pickle\nimport warnings\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n\n_allen2p_seed = 'x5cidj2hy87s'\nnp.random.seed(sum(ord(c) for c in _allen2p_seed) % 2**31)\n\nwarnings.filterwarnings(\"ignore\", message=\"Ignoring the following cached namespace\")\n\nimport allensdk.brain_observatory.behavior.behavior_project_cache as bpc\n\n# ---------- constants ----------\nN_LEVELS = 5            # discretization levels for running / pupil\nPROJECT_CODE = 'VisualBehavior'\n\nTRIAL_OUTCOMES = ['hit', 'miss', 'false_alarm', 'correct_reject']\n\n\ndef get_cache(cache_dir):\n    return bpc.VisualBehaviorOphysProjectCache.from_s3_cache(cache_dir=cache_dir)\n\n\ndef extract_session_data(bc, session_experiments):\n    \"\"\"Load all planes for one session, merge neural data, resample behavior.\"\"\"\n    datasets = {}\n    for exp_id in session_experiments.index:\n        datasets[exp_id] = bc.get_behavior_ophys_experiment(exp_id)\n\n    ref_ds = list(datasets.values())[0]\n    ophys_ts = ref_ds.ophys_timestamps\n\n    # --- Neural: merge dF/F across planes ---\n    dff_list = []\n    plane_labels = []\n    for exp_id, ds in datasets.items():\n        dff_list.append(np.vstack(ds.dff_traces.dff.values))\n        area = session_experiments.loc[exp_id, 'targeted_structure']\n        depth = session_experiments.loc[exp_id, 'imaging_depth']\n        plane_labels.extend([f'{area}_{depth}um'] * len(ds.dff_traces))\n    neural_data = np.vstack(dff_list)  # (N_neurons, T)\n\n    # --- Running speed: interpolate to ophys timebase ---\n    run = ref_ds.running_speed\n    f_run = interp1d(run['timestamps'].values, run['speed'].values,\n                     kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n    running_speed = f_run(ophys_ts)\n\n    # --- Pupil diameter: interpolate, exclude blinks ---\n    eye = ref_ds.eye_tracking\n    eye_clean = eye[~eye['likely_blink']]\n    f_pupil = interp1d(eye_clean['timestamps'].values,\n                       eye_clean['pupil_width'].values,\n                       kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n    pupil_diameter = f_pupil(ophys_ts)\n\n    # --- Trials table ---\n    trials_table = ref_ds.trials\n\n    return {\n        'ophys_ts': ophys_ts,\n        'neural_data': neural_data,\n        'plane_labels': plane_labels,\n        'running_speed': running_speed,\n        'pupil_diameter': pupil_diameter,\n        'trials_table': trials_table,\n    }\n\n\ndef segment_trials(session_data):\n    \"\"\"Segment into trials using the built-in trials table.\n\n    For each non-aborted, non-auto-rewarded trial with a valid change_time,\n    extract ophys frames from start_time to stop_time (variable length).\n\n    Returns list of trial dicts with raw (continuous) running/pupil and\n    per-frame image identity and change indicator.\n    \"\"\"\n    ophys_ts = session_data['ophys_ts']\n    neural_data = session_data['neural_data']\n    running_speed = session_data['running_speed']\n    pupil_diameter = session_data['pupil_diameter']\n    trials_table = session_data['trials_table']\n    T = len(ophys_ts)\n\n    # Skip aborted and auto-rewarded trials, and trials without a valid change_time\n    valid_trials = trials_table[\n        (~trials_table['aborted']) &\n        (~trials_table['auto_rewarded']) &\n        (trials_table['change_time'].notna())\n    ]\n\n    trials = []\n    for _, row in valid_trials.iterrows():\n        start_idx = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, row['start_time'])\n        end_idx = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, row['stop_time'])\n        if end_idx > T:\n            end_idx = T\n        if end_idx <= start_idx:\n            continue\n        idx = np.arange(start_idx, end_idx)\n        n_frames = len(idx)\n\n        # Determine trial outcome\n        outcome = 'other'\n        for label in TRIAL_OUTCOMES:\n            if row[label]:\n                outcome = label\n                break\n\n        # Time-varying image name: initial before change, change after\n        change_idx = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts[idx], row['change_time'])\n        image_names = np.empty(n_frames, dtype=object)\n        image_names[:change_idx] = row['initial_image_name']\n        image_names[change_idx:] = row['change_image_name']\n\n        # Binary image change indicator: 1 only during first flash + grey (750ms), go trials only\n        image_change = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=np.int8)\n        if row['go']:\n            change_end_idx = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts[idx], row['change_time'] + 0.75)\n            image_change[change_idx:change_end_idx] = 1\n\n        trials.append({\n            'image_names': image_names,              # (n_frames,) object array\n            'image_change': image_change,            # (n_frames,) int8\n            'trial_outcome': outcome,\n            'neural': neural_data[:, idx].astype(np.float32),\n            'running': running_speed[idx].astype(np.float32),\n            'pupil': pupil_diameter[idx].astype(np.float32),\n        })\n    return trials\n\n\ndef discretize(all_values_flat, n_levels=N_LEVELS):\n    \"\"\"Percentile-based discretization across all data.\"\"\"\n    valid = all_values_flat[~np.isnan(all_values_flat)]\n    percentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_levels + 1)\n    bin_edges = np.percentile(valid, percentiles)\n    return bin_edges\n\n\ndef apply_discretize(values, bin_edges):\n    \"\"\"Apply pre-computed bin edges. NaN -> 0 (safest discrete label).\"\"\"\n    out = np.digitize(values, bin_edges[1:-1]).astype(np.int8)\n    out[np.isnan(values)] = 0\n    return out\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description='Convert Allen Visual Behavior Ophys data to pickle format')\n    parser.add_argument('output', help='Output pickle file path')\n    parser.add_argument('--datadir', type=str,\n                        default='/groups/zhang/home/zhangl5/Allen_Brain',\n                        help='Allen SDK cache directory (default: /groups/zhang/home/zhangl5/Allen_Brain)')\n    mode = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n    mode.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True,\n                      help='Process all mice and sessions (default)')\n    mode.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true',\n                      help='Process only 2 mice with 2 sessions each for testing')\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    print(\"Loading Allen cache...\")\n    bc = get_cache(args.datadir)\n    experiment_table = bc.get_ophys_experiment_table()\n\n    # Filter to VisualBehavior project only\n    vb_experiments = experiment_table[experiment_table.project_code == PROJECT_CODE]\n\n    # The datalimit dataset holds a subset of the release, listed in\n    # DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv. edges[-1]=stop\n        centers=(edges[:-1]+edges[1:])/2\n        neu=aggregate_events(event_cumsum,ot,edges)\n        image=np.zeros(n,np.int64); change=np.zeros(n,np.int64)\n        # Only presentations overlapping the trial can contribute.\n        cand=presentations[(presentations.end_time>start)&(presentations.start_time<stop)]\n        for _,pr in cand.iterrows():\n            m=(centers>=float(pr.start_time))&(centers<float(pr.end_time))\n            image[m]=image_to_id[str(pr.image_name)]\n            if bool(pr.is_change) and start<=float(pr.start_time)<stop:\n                bi=min(n-1,max(0,int(np.floor((float(pr.start_time)-start)/BIN_S))))\n                change[bi]=1\n        run_cont=interp_valid(rt,rv,centers)\n        pupil_cont=interp_valid(pt,pupil,centers)\n        out=np.empty((5,n),np.int64)\n        out[0]=image; catch mean 37.85) |\n    91→| Go outcomes | 14,194 hit + 50,934 miss = 65,128 |\n    92→| Catch outcomes | 846 false alarm + 8,502 correct reject = 9,348 |\n    93→\n    94→### Available Variables and Types\n    95→- Trial-static: initial/change image, go/catch, hit/miss/false-alarm/correct-reject, aborted, auto-rewarded, trial start/stop/change times, response/reward times, lick arrays.\n    96→- Stimulus time-varying: image identity, onset/end, `is_change`, omitted, novelty, active/task block.\n    97→- Behavior time-varying: running speed (float), processed pupil area and ellipse dimensions (float), and blink validity (bool).\n    98→- Neural: precomputed dF/F and inferred/filtered event arrays (float, one value per ophys frame).\n    99→\n   100→---\n   101→\n   102→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   103→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   104→\n   105→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   106→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   107→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   108→| Behavioral cohort | 376 imaging sessions, 82 mice | Paper/methods: dataset \"contains behavior from 376 imaging sessions from 82 mice.\" |\n   109→| Focused excitatory cohort | 8,619 cells; 21 imaging sessions; 9 mice | Paper/methods neural results cohort. |\n   112→| Single-plane acquisition | 31 Hz | Whitepaper: 512x512 movies at 31 Hz for single plane. |\n   113→| Multiplane acquisition | 11 Hz per plane | Whitepaper: 512x512 movies at 11 Hz for each multiplane plane. |\n   114→| Stimulus cycle | 250 ms image + 500 ms gray = 750 ms | Paper task description. |\n   115→| Behavioral response window | 150-750 ms after change/sham change (before display-lag compensation) | Whitepaper/methods. |\n   116→| Change-time distribution | truncated exponential 2.25-8.25 s; mean about 4.2 s after flash alignment | Methods task description. |\n   117→| Catch fraction | about 12.5% in later matrix-sampling sessions | Methods task description. |\n   118→| Omission fraction | 5% of repeats | Paper task description. |\n   119→| Neural analysis representation | detected calcium events with time and magnitude | Paper/methods: \"For all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events.\" |\n   120→| Image-response interval | 50-800 ms after presentation | Paper methods uses 50 ms delay to account for signal propagation. |\n   121→| Published decoding interval | first 400 ms after image presentation | Paper Figure 6 caption. |\n   122→| Engagement threshold | reward rate >2 rewards/min | Methods behavioral processing. |\n   123→\n   124→### Processing Details\n   125→- The experiment is go/no-go visual change detection. A natural image appears for 250 ms followed by 500 ms gray; one image repeats until an identity change. Premature licking resets/delays the change and creates an aborted attempt.\n   126→- Go trials contain a real identity change; catch trials contain a sham change. Combined with behavior, requested outcomes are hit, miss, false alarm, and correct reject. Auto-rewarded and aborted trials are not valid choice trials.\n   127→- Behavioral analysis assigns events to each 750 ms image-presentation interval. Omitted flashes use the corresponding 750 ms interval.\n   128→- Reference neural analyses use inferred discrete calcium event magnitudes, not newly computed dF/F. Event extraction removes slow GCaMP decay dynamics. The whitepaper notes sampling-rate-aware noise estimation and compares 31 Hz to downsampled traces.\n   129→- For image response analyses, the paper uses 50-800 ms to account for neural latency. Its decoder uses the first 400 ms, concatenating each neuron's event samples per image.\n   130→- Running is open-loop and does not control task progression.\n   131→- Eye tracking fits pupil/eye/corneal-reflection ellipses. Processed `pupil_area` treats the observed ellipse major axis as the diameter of an underlying circular pupil. Frames marked likely blink (fit missing or area z-score >3, expanded by two adjacent frames) are represented as NaN.\n   132→- Single-plane and multiplane data have different native frame rates, so no fixed sample count can represent the same physical interval without temporal resampling/binning.\n   133→\n   134→### Curation Steps\n   135→\n   136→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   137→- Experiments undergo Allen Institute session QC. ROIs that are unions, duplicates/ghosts, non-cell objects, or otherwise invalid are excluded. AllenSDK `cell_specimen_table` already exposes only valid ROIs.\n   138→- The paper's focused biological analyses use selected familiar-image, multiplane sessions, but the decoder task requests the supplied Visual Behavior recordings generally; the implications are resolved in Steps 4-5.\n   139→\n   140→**Trial curation rules**:\n   141→- Include go and catch only; exclude aborted trials (lick before change) and auto-rewarded trials.\n   142→- Valid outcome classes are hit, miss, false alarm, and correct reject.\n   143→- Reference SDK performance metrics also exclude aborted trials.\n   144→\n   145→### Decoders Trained\n   146→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   147→|------------------|----------|\n   148→| Image change vs immediately preceding repeat | Cross-validated % correct shown graphically in Figure 6A; no exact numeric table in text. |\n   149→| Hit vs miss on image changes | Cross-validated % correct shown graphically in Figure 6C; no exact numeric table in text. |\n   150→\n   151→The paper used 5-fold cross-validated random forests and reports means/SEM over imaging planes. Its decoder tasks/windows differ from the required multi-output neural decoder, so these graphical results are qualitative alignment checks rather than exact expected accuracies.\n   152→\n   153→---\n   154→\n   155→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   156→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   157→\n   158→### Discrepancies Found\n   159→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   160→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   161→| Stimulus blocks | SDK warns to select block names containing `change_detection` | Each file also has gray screens and natural movie; active block is `change_detection_behavior`, passive is `change_detection_passive` | Paper analyzes task image presentations and states passive viewing was not analyzed | Keep active task sessions only and use `change_detection_behavior` rows. |\n   162→| Passive trial labels | SDK creates go/catch-style rows from replayed changes even without animal behavior | Passive sessions have all rows valid-looking and zero aborted trials; outcomes are dominated by no-response labels | Paper explicitly excludes passive viewing from its analyses; required trial outcome must reflect behavior | Exclude session types containing `passive`. |\n   163→| Session versus imaging plane | Cache table has one experiment per plane; multiscope sessions have 3-7 locally passing planes | Planes in a multiscope session share identical trial times and ophys timestamps but contain different cells/areas | Paper samples and reports decoding over imaging planes | Treat each `ophys_experiment_id` as one decoder session; do not merge planes or duplicate cells across areas. |\n   164→| Neural representation | SDK offers dF/F, raw events, and filtered events | All have one value per ophys frame | Paper says all neural analyses use detected calcium events | Use SDK `filtered_events`, preserving event magnitude and avoiding slow fluorescence decay. |\n   165→| Frame rate | SDK timestamps vary by rig | Single-plane approximately 31 Hz; multiscope approximately 11 Hz | Whitepaper confirms 31 Hz versus 11 Hz | Resample/bin by physical time onto one common bin width; never equate frame indices across rigs. |\n   166→| Pupil variable | SDK exposes processed area and ellipse dimensions, not a column named diameter | `pupil_area` is NaN around likely blinks | Whitepaper defines processed area from a circle whose diameter is ellipse major axis | Derive diameter as `2*sqrt(pupil_area/pi)` and preserve NaN until interpolation/validity handling. |\n   167→| Cohort sizes | Full project metadata has 1,936 experiments/107 mice | Supplied cache contains 284 experiment files; active subset has 202 experiments/174 ophys sessions/38 mice | Paper reports other analysis-specific cohorts (376 sessions/82 mice; smaller neural subsets) | Convert all locally supplied active task experiments; explicitly distinguish release, provided, and paper-specific cohorts. |\n   168→\n   169→### Final Consistent Understanding\n   170→- Input data are a curated local subset of the public release, not the exact focused cohort used for every paper figure.\n   171→- Active subset: 202 imaging-plane experiments, 174 unique behavioral/ophys sessions, 38 mice, and 29,444 valid experiment-cell records. Unique behavioral sessions contain 44,892 valid go/catch trials (39,265 go and 5,627 catch), with 14,194 hit, 25,071 miss, 845 false alarm, and 4,782 correct reject.\n   172→- Each experiment is independently decodable and has at least one curated imaging plane. Shared behavioral streams in multiscope experiments are expected, while neural populations differ.\n   173→- AllenSDK handles release loading, timestamp corrections, trial classification, ROI validity, event filtering, and blink-cleaned pupil area. Conversion should not duplicate these native processing steps.\n   174→\n   175→---\n   176→\n   177→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   178→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   179→\n   180→### Variable Mapping\n   181→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   182→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   183→| `events.filtered_events` + `ophys_timestamps` | `neural` | Sum detected event magnitudes in consecutive 100 ms physical-time bins within each trial | `BehaviorOphysExperiment.events`, `.ophys_timestamps` | Preserves event magnitude; common physical bin across 10.7/30.9 Hz rigs. |\n   184→| none | `input` | Empty float32 array of shape `(0, n_timepoints)` | N/A | Decoder task explicitly specifies no inputs. |\n   185→| task `stimulus_presentations.image_name/start_time/end_time` | `output[0]` image identity | 0=gray/no image; 1-16=the global sorted real image names; assign only bins whose centers fall within non-omitted image intervals | `BehaviorSession.stimulus_presentations` | `omitted` is gray/no image, not an image identity. |\n   186→| task `stimulus_presentations.is_change/start_time` | `output[1]` image change | 1 in the first 100 ms bin containing each real change onset, otherwise 0 | stimulus presentation table | A one-bin event pulse at identity change onset. |\n   187→| `running_speed.timestamps/speed` | `output[2]` running speed | Linear interpolation to bin centers, then discretize using experiment-wide quintile edges | `BehaviorSession.running_speed` | Five equal-percentile bins, labels 0-4. |\n   188→| processed `eye_tracking.pupil_area/timestamps` | `output[3]` pupil diameter | `2*sqrt(pupil_area/pi)`; interpolate over valid nonblink samples to bin centers; experiment-wide quintile edges | `BehaviorSession.eye_tracking` | Three sessions with no eye data are excluded; labels 0-4. |\n   189→| trial flags `hit/miss/false_alarm/correct_reject` | `output[4]` trial outcome | Static integer vector shape `(1,)`: hit=0, miss=1, false alarm=2, correct reject=3 | `Trials` / `Trial._get_trial_data` | Exactly one class per retained go/catch trial. |\n   190→| `mouse_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Sorted unique strings and per-experiment lookup | experiment metadata | Experiment/plane is target session unit. |\n   191→| `targeted_structure` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Global sorted regions; repeat experiment's area index for every cell | experiment metadata | Each supplied experiment is one imaging plane in one area. |\n   192→\n   193→### Key Decisions\n   194→1. **Use active sessions only**: Exclude session types containing `passive`, because replayed passive changes receive artificial no-response outcomes and the paper excludes passive data.\n   195→2. **Use one imaging plane per decoder session**: Matches paper decoding and preserves homogeneous neuron populations; multiscope planes legitimately share behavioral labels.\n   196→3. **Use SDK filtered events**: Matches the paper's detected-event neural representation and uses SDK sampling-rate-aware filtering rather than recomputing dF/F.\n   197→4. **Retain SDK go/catch trial boundaries**: Keep only `go | catch`, which inherently excludes aborted and auto-rewarded rows. Trial matrices may have variable timepoint counts, while all use the same 100 ms bins.\n   198→5. **100 ms bins**: This common physical bin has roughly 1 sample for multiscope and 3 for single-plane data, provides enough temporal resolution for 250 ms images and the one-bin change pulse, and greatly limits memory. Event values are summed (not averaged) so each bin represents detected event magnitude in that duration.\n   199→6. **No alignment-event cropping**: Trials are segmented at native `start_time` and `stop_time`; the required segmentation is trial-based, not a fixed event-centered analysis. Metadata alignment is trial start (`off_start=0`, variable `off_end=None`).\n   200→7. **Image gray class**: The required identity is the image presented during non-gray screen. Gray periods and omitted flashes are class 0. Sixteen image names across sets A/B are globally stable classes.\n   201→8. **Behavior quintiles are fitted per experiment**: This yields equal percentile bins within each recording and avoids cross-rig calibration/units differences. Repeated quantile edges are handled monotonically; digitized labels are clipped to 0-4.\n   202→9. **Pupil diameter and missing values**: Use whitepaper-defined processed pupil area. Interpolate only over SDK-valid points; do not use raw blink-contaminated values. Exclude the three active experiments with no eye samples (795953296, 833631914, 806456687), because an entire required output cannot be imputed responsibly.\n   203→10. **Trace edges**: Use bins fully anchored to trial start, with `ceil((stop-start)/0.1)` centers and clip final edge to stop. Nearest stream interpolation is not used for categorical stimuli; interval membership prevents onset smearing.\n   204→11. **Storage types**: Neural float32, empty input float32, categorical outputs int64, indices int64. This is validator-compatible and memory efficient.\n   205→\n   206→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   207→- [ ] Assert all traces per trial share exactly the same `n_timepoints` and all values are finite.\n   208→- [ ] Assert event aggregation against an independent direct timestamp mask using `np.allclose()` for selected neuron/trial/bin.\n   209→- [ ] Assert interpolated running/pupil values and resulting bins against independently loaded SDK streams using `np.allclose()`.\n   210→- [ ] Assert image interval labels/change pulses against raw SDK stimulus rows using `np.allclose()`.\n   211→- [ ] Assert retained trial count equals SDK `(go | catch).sum()` for every included experiment and outcomes sum to trial count.\n   212→- [ ] Assert each included session has at least two trials and one cell; excluded sessions are only passive or missing the mandatory pupil stream.\n   213→- [ ] Check global image classes, output ranges, quintile fractions, frame-rate groups, and subject/region mappings.\n   214→- [ ] Compare active/source and converted counts, accounting explicitly for multiplane duplication and three eye-data exclusions.\n   215→\n   216→### Efficiency Plan\n   217→- Load each experiment once through AllenSDK and construct all arrays in that pass.\n   218→- Precompute experiment-wide interpolated behavior and percentile edges once, not per trial.\n   219→- Use `searchsorted`, `reduceat`/bin indices, and interval slicing rather than per-timepoint Python loops.\n   220→- Initial exhaustive SDK scan took ~15 minutes for 202 active files; conversion should avoid its redundant table accesses and is expected to be similar or faster.\n   221→\n   222→---\n   223→\n   224→## Step 6: Script Development\n   225→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   226→\n   227→`/app/convert_data.py` was created and passes `python3 -m py_compile`. It supports `--full` (default behavior), `--sample`, and `--show-processing`, and writes the requested pickle structure. All experiment data are loaded through `VisualBehaviorOphysProjectCache.get_behavior_ophys_experiment`; no direct NWB reader is used.\n   228→\n   229→Implementation includes explicit assertions for event/timestamp lengths, finite values, exclusive outcomes, trial counts, and matching per-trial dimensions. Processing plots show event activity, image/change alignment, continuous and discretized running, continuous and discretized pupil, and event distributions.\n   230→\n   231→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   232→- AllenSDK object creation dominates runtime (~15 minutes for a prior exhaustive 202-file scan).\n   233→- Repeated per-bin/per-cell masks would be expensive, and retaining SDK objects would inflate memory.\n   234→\n   235→Code speedups added:\n   236→- Each experiment is loaded exactly once and all source tables/streams are reused.\n   237→- Event aggregation uses timestamp `searchsorted` plus cumulative sums across all cells.\n   238→- Continuous streams and percentile edges are prepared once per experiment.\n   239→- Only overlapping stimulus rows are iterated per trial; categorical labels use interval membership.\n   240→- Arrays are stored as float32/int64 and SDK experiment references are released after each session.\n   241→\n   242→---\n   243→\n   244→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   245→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   246→\n   247→### Sample Statistics\n   248→| Statistic | Value |\n   249→|-----------|-------|\n   250→| Neurons (experiment-cell records) | 101 |\n   251→| Neurons / session | 12, 89 |\n   252→| Subjects | 2 |\n   253→| Sessions / subject | 1 each |\n   254→| Trials (total) | 248 |\n   255→| Trials / session | 209, 39 |\n   256→| Timepoints / trial | 73-126 (100 ms bins) |\n   257→| Image identity distribution | gray 0.672; Stimulus intervals are half-open `[start,end)`. Change at an exact boundary maps by floor to the bin immediately after onset. Trials use `ceil(duration/bin)` so no tail is dropped. NWB files were **not** opened directly; all metadata and experiment contents were read via `VisualBehaviorOphysProjectCache`.\n- Full-release project tables exposed by the cache contain 1,936 experiments, 703 ophys/behavior sessions, 107 mice, and 133,066 valid cell records. Only the locally supplied 284 experiments are convertible without downloading data, so all conversion statistics below refer to that provided subset.\n- The local subset has 284 experiments from 247 ophys sessions (239 single-plane sessions; multiscope sessions contribute multiple simultaneous planes), 38 mice, and 42,147 experiment-cell records.\n- Areas are VISp (261 experiments) and VISl (23). Cre lines are Slc17a7-IRES2-Cre (153), Sst-IRES-Cre (85), and Vip-IRES-Cre (46). Experience levels are Familiar (150), Novel 1 (38), and Novel >1 (96).\n- Native experiment objects expose: `ophys_timestamps` (float seconds); `dff_traces` and inferred `events`/`filtered_events` arrays by valid cell; trial table; stimulus-presentation intervals; running speed (`timestamps`, `speed`); eye tracking (`timestamps`, processed/raw areas, ellipse width/height, blink flag); and metadata.\n- Representative experiment 775614751: 89 valid cells, 149,472 neural frames, median frame interval 0.03231 s (~31 Hz), 1,117 trial attempts, 13,793 stimulus rows, 287,868 running samples, and 144,962 eye samples. Neural trace/event lengths exactly equal ophys timestamp length.\n- Stimulus tables contain multiple blocks (task, gray screen, natural movie). Task rows are identified by `stimulus_block_name` containing `change_detection`; representative task images included eight image identities plus omitted flashes.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (experiment-cell records, local) | 42,147 |\n| Neurons / experiment | min 4; median 66; mean 148.40; max 666 |\n| Subjects | 38 |\n| Sessions / subject | 247 sessions across 38 mice (mean 6.50) |\n| Ophys sessions | 247 |\n| Ophys experiments / planes | 284 |\n| Trials (all attempts) | 148,231 |\n| Requested valid trials (go + catch) | 74,476 |\n| Valid trials / session | mean 301.52 (go mean 263.68; 21 imaging sessions; 9 mice | Paper/methods neural results cohort. |\n| Focused Sst cohort | 470 cells; 15 imaging sessions; 6 mice | Paper/methods neural results cohort. |\n| Focused Vip cohort | 1,239 cells; 21 imaging sessions; 9 mice | Paper/methods neural results cohort. |\n| Single-plane acquisition | 31 Hz | Whitepaper: 512x512 movies at 31 Hz for single plane. |\n| Multiplane acquisition | 11 Hz per plane | Whitepaper: 512x512 movies at 11 Hz for each multiplane plane. |\n| Stimulus cycle | 250 ms image + 500 ms gray = 750 ms | Paper task description. |\n| Behavioral response window | 150-750 ms after change/sham change (before display-lag compensation) | Whitepaper/methods. |\n| Change-time distribution | truncated exponential 2.25-8.25 s; mean about 4.2 s after flash alignment | Methods task description. |\n| Catch fraction | about 12.5% in later matrix-sampling sessions | Methods task description. |\n| Omission fraction | 5% of repeats | Paper task description. |\n| Neural analysis representation | detected calcium events with time and magnitude | Paper/methods: \"For all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events.\" |\n| Image-response interval | 50-800 ms after presentation | Paper methods uses 50 ms delay to account for signal propagation. |\n| Published decoding interval | first 400 ms after image presentation | Paper Figure 6 caption. |\n| Engagement threshold | reward rate >2 rewards/min | Methods behavioral processing. |\n\n### Processing Details\n- The experiment is go/no-go visual change detection. A natural image appears for 250 ms followed by 500 ms gray; one image repeats until an identity change. Premature licking resets/delays the change and creates an aborted attempt.\n- Go trials contain a real identity change; catch trials contain a sham change. Combined with behavior, requested outcomes are hit, miss, false alarm, and correct reject. Auto-rewarded and aborted trials are not valid choice trials.\n- Behavioral analysis assigns events to each 750 ms image-presentation interval. Omitted flashes use the corresponding 750 ms interval.\n- Reference neural analyses use inferred discrete calcium event magnitudes, not newly computed dF/F. Event extraction removes slow GCaMP decay dynamics. The whitepaper notes sampling-rate-aware noise estimation and compares 31 Hz to downsampled traces.\n- For image response analyses, the paper uses 50-800 ms to account for neural latency. Its decoder uses the first 400 ms, concatenating each neuron's event samples per image.\n- Running is open-loop and does not control task progression.\n- Eye tracking fits pupil/eye/corneal-reflection ellipses. Processed `pupil_area` treats the observed ellipse major axis as the diameter of an underlying circular pupil. Frames marked likely blink (fit missing or area z-score >3, expanded by two adjacent frames) are represented as NaN.\n- Single-plane and multiplane data have different native frame rates, so no fixed sample count can represent the same physical interval without temporal resampling/binning.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Experiments undergo Allen Institute session QC. ROIs that are unions, duplicates/ghosts, non-cell objects, or otherwise invalid are excluded. AllenSDK `cell_specimen_table` already exposes only valid ROIs.\n- The paper's focused biological analyses use selected familiar-image, multiplane sessions, but the decoder task requests the supplied Visual Behavior recordings generally; the implications are resolved in Steps 4-5.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Include go and catch only; exclude aborted trials (lick before change) and auto-rewarded trials.\n- Valid outcome classes are hit, miss, false alarm, and correct reject.\n- Reference SDK performance metrics also exclude aborted trials.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|------------------|----------|\n| Image change vs immediately preceding repeat | Cross-validated % correct shown graphically in Figure 6A; no exact numeric table in text. |\n| Hit vs miss on image changes | Cross-validated % correct shown graphically in Figure 6C; no exact numeric table in text. |\n\nThe paper used 5-fold cross-validated random forests and reports means/SEM over imaging planes. Its decoder tasks/windows differ from the required multi-output neural decoder, so these graphical results are qualitative alignment checks rather than exact expected accuracies.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Stimulus blocks | SDK warns to select block names containing `change_detection` | Each file also has gray screens and natural movie; active block is `change_detection_behavior`, passive is `change_detection_passive` | Paper analyzes task image presentations and states passive viewing was not analyzed | Keep active task sessions only and use `change_detection_behavior` rows. |\n| Passive trial labels | SDK creates go/catch-style rows from replayed changes even without animal behavior | Passive sessions have all rows valid-looking and zero aborted trials; outcomes are dominated by no-response labels | Paper explicitly excludes passive viewing from its analyses; required trial outcome must reflect behavior | Exclude session types containing `passive`. |\n| Session versus imaging plane | Cache table has one experiment per plane; multiscope sessions have 3-7 locally passing planes | Planes in a multiscope session share identical trial times and ophys timestamps but contain different cells/areas | Paper samples and reports decoding over imaging planes | Treat each `ophys_experiment_id` as one decoder session; do not merge planes or duplicate cells across areas. |\n| Neural representation | SDK offers dF/F, raw events, and filtered events | All have one value per ophys frame | Paper says all neural analyses use detected calcium events | Use SDK `filtered_events`, preserving event magnitude and avoiding slow fluorescence decay. |\n| Frame rate | SDK timestamps vary by rig | Single-plane approximately 31 Hz; multiscope approximately 11 Hz | Whitepaper confirms 31 Hz versus 11 Hz | Resample/bin by physical time onto one common bin width; never equate frame indices across rigs. |\n| Pupil variable | SDK exposes processed area and ellipse dimensions, not a column named diameter | `pupil_area` is NaN around likely blinks | Whitepaper defines processed area from a circle whose diameter is ellipse major axis | Derive diameter as `2*sqrt(pupil_area/pi)` and preserve NaN until interpolation/validity handling. |\n| Cohort sizes | Full project metadata has 1,936 experiments/107 mice | Supplied cache contains 284 experiment files; active subset has 202 experiments/174 ophys sessions/38 mice | Paper reports other analysis-specific cohorts (376 sessions/82 mice; smaller neural subsets) | Convert all locally supplied active task experiments; explicitly distinguish release, provided, and paper-specific cohorts. |\n\n### Final Consistent Understanding\n- Input data are a curated local subset of the public release, not the exact focused cohort used for every paper figure.\n- Active subset: 202 imaging-plane experiments, 174 unique behavioral/ophys sessions, 38 mice, and 29,444 valid experiment-cell records. Unique behavioral sessions contain 44,892 valid go/catch trials (39,265 go and 5,627 catch), with 14,194 hit, 25,071 miss, 845 false alarm, and 4,782 correct reject.\n- Each experiment is independently decodable and has at least one curated imaging plane. Shared behavioral streams in multiscope experiments are expected, while neural populations differ.\n- AllenSDK handles release loading, timestamp corrections, trial classification, ROI validity, event filtering, and blink-cleaned pupil area. Conversion should not duplicate these native processing steps.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `events.filtered_events` + `ophys_timestamps` | `neural` | Sum detected event magnitudes in consecutive 100 ms physical-time bins within each trial | `BehaviorOphysExperiment.events`, `.ophys_timestamps` | Preserves event magnitude; common physical bin across 10.7/30.9 Hz rigs. |\n| none | `input` | Empty float32 array of shape `(0, n_timepoints)` | N/A | Decoder task explicitly specifies no inputs. |\n| task `stimulus_presentations.image_name/start_time/end_time` | `output[0]` image identity | 0=gray/no image; 1-16=the global sorted real image names; assign only bins whose centers fall within non-omitted image intervals | `BehaviorSession.stimulus_presentations` | `omitted` is gray/no image, not an image identity. |\n| task `stimulus_presentations.is_change/start_time` | `output[1]` image change | 1 in the first 100 ms bin containing each real change onset, otherwise 0 | stimulus presentation table | A one-bin event pulse at identity change onset. |\n| `running_speed.timestamps/speed` | `output[2]` running speed | Linear interpolation to bin centers, then discretize using experiment-wide quintile edges | `BehaviorSession.running_speed` | Five equal-percentile bins, labels 0-4. |\n| processed `eye_tracking.pupil_area/timestamps` | `output[3]` pupil diameter | `2*sqrt(pupil_area/pi)`; interpolate over valid nonblink samples to bin centers; experiment-wide quintile edges | `BehaviorSession.eye_tracking` | Three sessions with no eye data are excluded; labels 0-4. |\n| trial flags `hit/miss/false_alarm/correct_reject` | `output[4]` trial outcome | Static integer vector shape `(1,)`: hit=0, miss=1, false alarm=2, correct reject=3 | `Trials` / `Trial._get_trial_data` | Exactly one class per retained go/catch trial. |\n| `mouse_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Sorted unique strings and per-experiment lookup | experiment metadata | Experiment/plane is target session unit. |\n| `targeted_structure` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Global sorted regions; repeat experiment's area index for every cell | experiment metadata | Each supplied experiment is one imaging plane in one area. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use active sessions only**: Exclude session types containing `passive`, because replayed passive changes receive artificial no-response outcomes and the paper excludes passive data.\n2. **Use one imaging plane per decoder session**: Matches paper decoding and preserves homogeneous neuron populations; multiscope planes legitimately share behavioral labels.\n3. **Use SDK filtered events**: Matches the paper's detected-event neural representation and uses SDK sampling-rate-aware filtering rather than recomputing dF/F.\n4. **Retain SDK go/catch trial boundaries**: Keep only `go | catch`, which inherently excludes aborted and auto-rewarded rows. Trial matrices may have variable timepoint counts, while all use the same 100 ms bins.\n5. **100 ms bins**: This common physical bin has roughly 1 sample for multiscope and 3 for single-plane data, provides enough temporal resolution for 250 ms images and the one-bin change pulse, and greatly limits memory. Event values are summed (not averaged) so each bin represents detected event magnitude in that duration.\n6. **No alignment-event cropping**: Trials are segmented at native `start_time` and `stop_time`; the required segmentation is trial-based, not a fixed event-centered analysis. Metadata alignment is trial start (`off_start=0`, variable `off_end=None`).\n7. **Image gray class**: The required identity is the image presented during non-gray screen. Gray periods and omitted flashes are class 0. Sixteen image names across sets A/B are globally stable classes.\n8. **Behavior quintiles are fitted per experiment**: This yields equal percentile bins within each recording and avoids cross-rig calibration/units differences. Repeated quantile edges are handled monotonically; digitized labels are clipped to 0-4.\n9. **Pupil diameter and missing values**: Use whitepaper-defined processed pupil area. Interpolate only over SDK-valid points; do not use raw blink-contaminated values. Exclude the three active experiments with no eye samples (795953296, 833631914, 806456687), because an entire required output cannot be imputed responsibly.\n10. **Trace edges**: Use bins fully anchored to trial start, with `ceil((stop-start)/0.1)` centers and clip final edge to stop. Nearest stream interpolation is not used for categorical stimuli; interval membership prevents onset smearing.\n11. **Storage types**: Neural float32, empty input float32, categorical outputs int64, indices int64. This is validator-compatible and memory efficient.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Assert all traces per trial share exactly the same `n_timepoints` and all values are finite.\n- [ ] Assert event aggregation against an independent direct timestamp mask using `np.allclose()` for selected neuron/trial/bin.\n- [ ] Assert interpolated running/pupil values and resulting bins against independently loaded SDK streams using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Assert image interval labels/change pulses against raw SDK stimulus rows using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Assert retained trial count equals SDK `(go | catch).sum()` for every included experiment and outcomes sum to trial count.\n- [ ] Assert each included session has at least two trials and one cell; excluded sessions are only passive or missing the mandatory pupil stream.\n- [ ] Check global image classes, output ranges, quintile fractions, frame-rate groups, and subject/region mappings.\n- [ ] Compare active/source and converted counts, accounting explicitly for multiplane duplication and three eye-data exclusions.\n\n### Efficiency Plan\n- Load each experiment once through AllenSDK and construct all arrays in that pass.\n- Precompute experiment-wide interpolated behavior and percentile edges once, not per trial.\n- Use `searchsorted`, `reduceat`/bin indices, and interval slicing rather than per-timepoint Python loops.\n- Initial exhaustive SDK scan took ~15 minutes for 202 active files; conversion should avoid its redundant table accesses and is expected to be similar or faster.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` was created and passes `python3 -m py_compile`. It supports `--full` (default behavior), `--sample`, and `--show-processing`, and writes the requested pickle structure. All experiment data are loaded through `VisualBehaviorOphysProjectCache.get_behavior_ophys_experiment`; no direct NWB reader is used.\n\nImplementation includes explicit assertions for event/timestamp lengths, finite values, exclusive outcomes, trial counts, and matching per-trial dimensions. Processing plots show event activity, image/change alignment, continuous and discretized running, continuous and discretized pupil, and event distributions.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- AllenSDK object creation dominates runtime (~15 minutes for a prior exhaustive 202-file scan).\n- Repeated per-bin/per-cell masks would be expensive, and retaining SDK objects would inflate memory.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Each experiment is loaded exactly once and all source tables/streams are reused.\n- Event aggregation uses timestamp `searchsorted` plus cumulative sums across all cells.\n- Continuous streams and percentile edges are prepared once per experiment.\n- Only overlapping stimulus rows are iterated per trial; categorical labels use interval membership.\n- Arrays are stored as float32/int64 and SDK experiment references are released after each session.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (experiment-cell records) | 101 |\n| Neurons / session | 12, 89 |\n| Subjects | 2 |\n| Sessions / subject | 1 each |\n| Trials (total) | 248 |\n| Trials / session | 209, 39 |\n| Timepoints / trial | 73-126 (100 ms bins) |\n| Image identity distribution | gray 0.672; Expanding the pulse would violate the instruction to use value 1 \"right after\" a change and would distort timing.\n- **Running and pupil** are five-way within-session percentile labels. The AI justified this by noting that the paper's decoding analyses are performed per imaging plane, and that each plane has a distinct neural population. Multiscope sessions legitimately share behavioral labels across planes.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are defined using the SDK's trials table. Each trial spans `start_time` to `stop_time` with variable-length duration, rebinned into 100ms time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrials=exp.trials\ntrials=trials[trials.go.astype(bool)|trials.catch.astype(bool)].copy()\nif len(trials)<2: raise ValueError('fewer than two valid trials')\n```\n\n```python\nfor ti,(_,tr) in enumerate(trials.iterrows()):\n    start=float(tr.start_time); stop=float(tr.stop_time)\n    n=max(1,int(np.ceil((stop-start)/BIN_S)))\n    edges=start+np.arange(n+1)*BIN_S; edges[-1]=stop\n```\n\niii. The AI uses the SDK's go/catch flags, which are mutually exclusive with aborted and auto-rewarded. The variable-length trial window captures both pre-change stimulus flashes and the post-change response window.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Trials are filtered by: (1) only go|catch trials retained, (2) sessions with <2 valid trials raise an error and are skipped, (3) passive sessions are excluded at the experiment level, (4) 3 experiments with missing eye tracking are excluded. The trial outcome flags are validated as mutually exclusive.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrials=trials[trials.go.astype(bool)|trials.catch.astype(bool)].copy()\nif len(trials)<2: raise ValueError('fewer than two valid trials')\n```\n\n```python\ntab=tab[~tab.session_type.str.contains('passive',case=False,na=False)]\ntab=tab[~tab.index.isin(MISSING_EYE_IDS)]\n```\n\n```python\ndef outcome_value(row):\n    vals=[bool(row[k]) for k in OUTCOMES]\n    if sum(vals)!=1: raise ValueError(f'non-exclusive outcome flags {vals}')\n```\n\niii. The go|catch filter matches the instructions to include Go and Catch trials while excluding Aborted and Auto-rewarded. Passive sessions are excluded because their replayed changes lack meaningful behavioral outcomes. Missing eye tracking sessions are excluded because pupil diameter is a required output.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `exp.events.filtered_events` — the SDK's inferred and filtered calcium event magnitudes, not dF/F traces.\n\nii.\n```python\nev=exp.events\ntraces=np.stack(ev.filtered_events.to_numpy()).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI justified this by citing the paper: \"For all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events.\" The filtered events remove slow GCaMP decay dynamics and are recommended by the SDK for neural analysis. This matches the paper's methodology.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Filtered event magnitudes are summed within 100ms time bins using a cumulative-sum-based aggregation. Bin edges are computed from trial start to stop in 100ms increments, with the final bin edge clipped to the trial stop time.\n\nii.\n```python\nevent_cumsum=np.concatenate([np.zeros((traces.shape[0],1),np.float32),\n                              np.cumsum(traces,dtype=np.float32,axis=1)],axis=1)\n```\n\n```python\ndef aggregate_events(event_cumsum, timestamps, edges):\n    lo=np.searchsorted(timestamps,edges[:-1],side='left')\n    hi=np.searchsorted(timestamps,edges[1:],side='left')\n    return (event_cumsum[:,hi]-event_cumsum[:,lo]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The cumulative sum approach is efficient (O(n) per experiment rather than O(n*T) per trial). Summing event magnitudes in bins preserves total detected activity per bin. The 100ms bin size was chosen to provide a common temporal grid across different frame rates (31 Hz single-plane and 11 Hz multiscope).\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. No additional quality filtering is applied beyond the SDK's built-in curation. The SDK's `cell_specimen_table` and `events` already expose only valid ROIs. The code asserts that all values are finite.\n\nii.\n```python\nif traces.shape[1]!=len(ot): raise ValueError('event/timestamp length mismatch')\nif not np.isfinite(traces).all(): raise ValueError('nonfinite filtered events')\n```\n\niii. The SDK pipeline already applies quality control (cell segmentation, ROI validation, neuropil correction). No further filtering was deemed necessary.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Each trial's neural data is aligned to the trial's `start_time` from the SDK trials table. The 100ms bin edges are computed as `start + k*0.1` for k=0,...,n, with the final edge clipped to `stop_time`. Neural events are aggregated within these bins using the ophys timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nstart=float(tr.start_time); stop=float(tr.stop_time)\nn=max(1,int(np.ceil((stop-start)/BIN_S)))\nedges=start+np.arange(n+1)*BIN_S; edges[-1]=stop\ncenters=(edges[:-1]+edges[1:])/2\nneu=aggregate_events(event_cumsum,ot,edges)\n```\n\niii. This matches the paper's methodology.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Filtered event magnitudes are summed within 100ms time bins using a cumulative-sum-based aggregation. Bin edges are computed from trial start to stop in 100ms increments, with the final bin edge clipped to the trial stop time.\n\nii.\n```python\nevent_cumsum=np.concatenate([np.zeros((traces.shape[0],1),np.float32),\n                              np.cumsum(traces,dtype=np.float32,axis=1)],axis=1)\n```\n\n```python\ndef aggregate_events(event_cumsum, timestamps, edges):\n    lo=np.searchsorted(timestamps,edges[:-1],side='left')\n    hi=np.searchsorted(timestamps,edges[1:],side='left')\n    return (event_cumsum[:,hi]-event_cumsum[:,lo]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The cumulative sum approach is efficient (O(n) per experiment rather than O(n*T) per trial). Summing event magnitudes in bins preserves total detected activity per bin. The 100ms bin size was chosen to provide a common temporal grid across different frame rates (31 Hz single-plane and 11 Hz multiscope).\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. No additional quality filtering is applied beyond the SDK's built-in curation. The SDK's `cell_specimen_table` and `events` already expose only valid ROIs. The code asserts that all values are finite.\n\nii.\n```python\nif traces.shape[1]!=len(ot): raise ValueError('event/timestamp length mismatch')\nif not np.isfinite(traces).all(): raise ValueError('nonfinite filtered events')\n```\n\niii. The SDK pipeline already applies quality control (cell segmentation, ROI validation, neuropil correction). No further filtering was deemed necessary.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Each trial's neural data is aligned to the trial's `start_time` from the SDK trials table. The 100ms bin edges are computed as `start + k*0.1` for k=0,...,n, with the final edge clipped to `stop_time`. Neural events are aggregated within these bins using the ophys timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nstart=float(tr.start_time); stop=float(tr.stop_time)\nn=max(1,int(np.ceil((stop-start)/BIN_S)))\nedges=start+np.arange(n+1)*BIN_S; edges[-1]=stop\ncenters=(edges[:-1]+edges[1:])/2\nneu=aggregate_events(event_cumsum,ot,edges)\n```\n\niii. Trial start serves as the temporal anchor. Using bin edges aligned to absolute time and aggregating events by timestamp ensures correct alignment regardless of frame rate.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The data is rebinned to 100ms time bins. This is a fixed physical-time bin applied uniformly across all experiments, regardless of native frame rate.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_S = 0.1\n```\n\n```python\n'time_bin_size':100.0\n```\n\niii. The AI justified 100ms bins as necessary because the dataset includes both single-plane (~31 Hz, ~32ms frames) and multiscope (~11 Hz, ~93ms frames) recordings. A common physical time bin ensures consistent temporal resolution across all sessions, as required by the format specification (\"Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions\").\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Image identity* derived from?\n\ni. Image identity is derived from the `stimulus_presentations` table, specifically the `image_name`, `start_time`, and `end_time` columns.
